The test test_column_totals_ignores_text_cells expects it to ignore text cells and only sum numeric ones, returning [\"\", 3] for [[\"a\", 1], [\"b\", 2]]. But the current implementation will throw an error trying to sum strings. However, that test exists, so maybe the actual code has been updated? Or maybe the test is wrong? Let's see if there's more to totals.py. It only has column_totals function.\n\nNow, the issue: \"the export is missing the totals row\". The test test_export_appends_totals_row expects export_csv with totals=True to include a totals row. Currently, export.py's export_csv doesn't handle totals argument. Also render_rows doesn't handle marking last row as total or computing totals.\n\nWe need to modify the code to include totals row when requested. Likely we need to:\n\n1. Compute column totals using column_totals function.\n2. Append a row that says \"TOTAL\" followed by the totals? The expected output in the test is \"TOTAL,4,6\". That is, the row starts with \"TOTAL\" and then the sum of each column: 4 and 6.\n\nSo the export_csv function should, when totals=True, append a row where the first cell is \"TOTAL\" and the rest are the column totals.\n\nBut currently export_csv takes only rows. We need to modify its signature to accept an optional totals parameter, or check if a second argument is passed. Looking at the test call: export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True). That suggests the function is called with two arguments: rows and totals. But the implementation only accepts one argument. So we need to adjust the function to accept more arguments, or use *args, or check if there's a second parameter.\n\nAlternatively, maybe the export_csv function is supposed to be called with render_rows having some flag. But the test expects export_csv to handle totals itself.\n\nLet's look at the test file again. It has:\n\ndef test_export_appends_totals_row():\n    assert export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) == \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\"\n\nSo the test expects export_csv to be called with two positional or keyword arguments? It uses totals=True as a keyword argument. So the function definition should support that.\n\nThus, we need to edit export.py to modify export_csv to accept a totals parameter defaulting to False, and if True, append a totals row.\n\nAlso, we need to compute the totals row. We have column_totals function in totals.py that computes column totals for numeric columns. But the expected totals row is \"TOTAL,4,6\". That is, the first column is the string \"TOTAL\", and the remaining columns are the totals of each column. However, column_totals currently returns [4, 6] for the rows [[1,2],[3,4]]. That matches the numeric totals. So we can use column_totals(rows) to get the list of totals per column. Then we need to prepend \"TOTAL\" to that list.\n\nBut column_totals as implemented will fail if there are non-numeric cells. However, the test test_column_totals_ignores_text_cells expects it to handle mixed types. That suggests column_totals should be smarter. But that's a separate issue. For now, we just need to fix the export missing totals row.\n\nWe should also check if render_rows is used elsewhere. The current export_csv uses render_rows(rows) to render each row as comma-separated values. If we want to add a totals row, we could either:\n\n- Call render_rows on the original rows, then compute totals row string manually and append.\n- Modify render_rows to accept an option to mark last row as total, but that seems more complex.\n\nSimpler: In export_csv, if totals=True, compute totals row and append after the regular rows.\n\nSo edit export.py:\n\nCurrent code:\n\nfrom render import render_rows\n\ndef export_csv(rows):\n    return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n\nWe need to change it to:\n\nfrom render import render_rows\nfrom totals import column_totals\n\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    lines = render_rows(rows)\n    if totals:\n        # Compute column totals\n        col_totals = column_totals(rows)\n        # Create totals row: start with \"TOTAL\", then the totals\n        totals_row = [\"TOTAL\"] + [str(t) for t in col_totals]\n        lines.append(\",\".join(totals_row))\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\nBut we need to ensure column_totals works with the data. For now, assume it works for numeric data.\n\nAlternatively, we could use the existing test_render_marks_last_row_as_total which suggests there is a way to mark last row as total via render_rows with mark_last=True. But render_rows currently doesn't accept mark_last. Maybe render_rows has an optional second argument? Let's check render.py again. It only has:\n\ndef render_rows(rows):\n    return [\",\".join(str(v) for v in r) for r in rows]\n\nNo parameters. So that test might be broken or there is another render_rows function not shown? But we only have this file.\n\nGiven the simplicity, I think modifying export_csv to handle totals parameter is the right approach.\n\nNow, we need to run the tests again.
